  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Washington, leader of America (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: America
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Minuteman, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Monitor, Replacing Ironclad (Requires technology: Industrialization)
        - Unique Building: Independence Hall, Replacing Royal Collection (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Building: Homestead, Replacing Stable (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Civilization Ability: Military Land Units start with the Sentry Promotion. Tile purchase cost is reduced by 25% and is reset upon founding a City. +20 Production when purchasing tiles, scaling with Era. Can purchase tiles owned by others.
      - Leader: Washington
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      
      ## Policies
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 6
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 4
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 5
        - Boldness: 4
        - WarBias: 4
        - HostileBias: 7
        - WarmongerHate: 8
        - NeutralBias: 4
        - FriendlyBias: 9
        - GuardedBias: 3
        - AfraidBias: 5
        - DiplomaticBalance: 8
        - Friendliness: 9
        - WorkWithWillingness: 9
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 5
        - Loyalty: 7
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 9
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 3
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 5
        - MinorCivWarBias: 2
        - DenounceWillingness: 2
        - Forgiveness: 2
        - Meanness: 3
        - Neediness: 7
        - Chattiness: 1
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (Reducing Boldness from 5 to 4 and WarBias from 5 to 4 in response to India's peace treaty expiring with their overwhelming military advantage. We must be more cautious and avoid provoking additional conflicts until we can strengthen our forces. VictoryCompetitiveness reduced from 7 to 6 - survival takes priority over competitive victory pursuit right now. DiplomaticBalance reduced from 8 to 7 as defensive preparations become more critical. WorkWithWillingness increased to 9 from 8 - we need maximum cooperation from allies Netherlands and Polynesia. HostileBias reduced from 8 to 7 to avoid escalation. Friendliness remains 9 to maintain critical alliances. Meanness at 3, not aggressive given our military disadvantage. Chattiness at 1 to avoid drawing unwanted attention.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Nuclear Fission
        - Rationale: Selecting Nuclear Fission (unlocks Atomic Bombs, Nuclear Plants) after Rocketry completes. India's overwhelming military superiority (4.5x our strength) makes nuclear deterrence our only viable survival option. Atomic Bombs are essential as equalizers against conventional forces. Nuclear Plants boost production for military units and spaceship components. However, this research is dependent on acquiring Uranium (currently 0) through diplomacy with Netherlands (4 Uranium) or Portugal (2 Uranium). Researching Nuclear Fission first preserves Nuclear Fission-specific buildings while we work on Uranium acquisition.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Elite Forces (Policy)
        - Rationale: Selecting Elite Forces (Level 1 Autocracy Tenet) as next policy. Provides +15 Experience for newly created Military Units and 50% more Experience from combat. This is invaluable for building stronger defensive forces quickly against India's overwhelming military. The experience bonuses help our units gain promotions faster, improving combat effectiveness and survival rates despite numerical disadvantage. With America fighting Poland (captured San Francisco), Persia (ongoing war), and now obligated to defend against India, having higher-quality units with better promotions becomes critical for successful defense. Elite Forces synergizes with our military-focused Autocracy ideology and supports desperate current wars.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### America
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Maintaining alliance with Polynesia unchanged. Polynesia remains America's strongest ally despite being Unhappy (48%). Their 8 World Congress delegates are crucial, and they continue fighting Persia (War Score: 24% in their favor). Positive relationship ensures diplomatic support in the upcoming World Congress vote (Treasure Fleet proposal by Polynesia). Trade relationships and open borders remain active. No change from current strong baseline.
        - UpdatedTurn: 213
        
        ### Portugal
        - Public: 5
        - Private: 5
        - Rationale: Maintaining positive relationship with Portugal (unchanged at 5/5). Portugal is an Industrial-era power with 7 delegates, strong military (1509 strength), and extensive trade network. Their defensive pact with India and DoF with The Netherlands makes them geopolitically significant. The modest positive signal maintains diplomatic stability while avoiding overcommitment to a potential rival. Current relationship supports peaceful coexistence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 264
        
        ### Valletta
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Strong negative relationship toward Valletta after their declaration of war on America and Egypt. This city-state allied with India and now attacks both us and our ally. Setting Public -50 and Private -30 signals our strong hostility and guides our AI to prioritize Valletta as a target. This aggressive modifier helps coordinate military response against the new threat while ensuring we don't waste diplomatic resources on reconciliation.
        - UpdatedTurn: 311
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 25
        - Rationale: Strengthening relationship with Egypt (Private from 20 to 25, Public unchanged at 20). Egypt is our key defensive pact ally fighting multiple fronts. Despite losing Alexandria to India and being Unhappy, Egypt remains committed. India has proposed sanctioning Egypt in World Congress. Strengthening this relationship signals support and encourages Egypt to maintain pressure on Poland and India. Egypt's military is weak (687) so diplomatic solidarity is crucial.
        - UpdatedTurn: 315
        
        ### Ormus
        - Public: -10
        - Private: -10
        - Rationale: Setting negative relationship with Ormus after their declaration of war on America and Egypt. While Ormus is a minor city-state (no strategic value), this signals our resolve to respond to aggression and guides AI to prioritize Ormus as a hostile actor in the ongoing wars against India and their allies. The moderate negative values reflect that this is a minor threat compared to Poland/India.
        - UpdatedTurn: 331
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: -40
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Significantly worsening relationship with Poland after their capture of San Francisco (Smithsonian Institution wonder lost, valuable Oil/Aluminum resources taken, 14 population city captured). Poland's Winged Hussars and Landships continue aggressive operations. Public penalty increased to -40 and Private to -30 signals our determination to recover the city and respond militarily. This hostile modifier will guide AI for sustained conflict until San Francisco is recaptured or adequate compensation achieved. The relationship deterioration reflects the severity of this territorial loss and Poland's escalating warmongering behavior (now controlling both Thebes and San Francisco).
        - UpdatedTurn: 340
        
        ### Persia
        - Public: -35
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: Worsening relationship with Persia after Ergili's capture. While we're winning this war (War Score: 42%), Persia still controls valuable territory and potentially holds antigues/resources. More hostile stance (Public -30→-35, Private -20→-25) signals our intent to continue pressing military advantage while Persia remains weakened. Persia attacked unprovoked with Poland and India, and this stronger hostile modifier helps AI maintain aggressive posture toward Persia for potential additional gains before peace negotiations. Persia is now the weakest enemy (725 strength) and we should capitalize on this opportunity.
        - UpdatedTurn: 342
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: 55
        - Private: 40
        - Rationale: Further strengthening relationship with Polynesia (Public 52→55, Private 38→40). With India attacking The Netherlands, Polynesia becomes more important as a counterweight. Polynesia has 14 delegates, Order ideology like India (may have diplomatic influence), and is at war with Persia (our enemy). Stronger ties may encourage them to pressure India diplomatically or support The Netherlands in World Congress, given India's aggressive behavior threatens the entire alliance network.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### India
        - Public: -45
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: India declared war on The Netherlands, triggering America's defensive pact obligation. Significantly worsening relationship from -35/-25 to -45/-30 as this is now an active war threatening our strategic ally network. India's military (19,745) is 4.5x America's (4,419), making them an existential threat. The stronger hostility signal guides AI to treat India as primary threat and coordinate defensive operations with The Netherlands. Maintaining some restraint (not -100/-100) to avoid India focusing all aggression solely on America given the power disparity and our need for preparation time. This escalation reflects the dire strategic reality created by India's declaration of war on our ally.
        - UpdatedTurn: 355
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: 45
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Strengthening alliance with The Netherlands from 40/40 to 45/45 after India's declaration of war. They are now under direct attack from our greatest threat, and our defensive pact makes this a shared conflict. The Netherlands has 23 World Congress delegates (51% victory progress) - their survival and cooperation is critical for diplomatic victory prospects and balancing India's power. Stronger support signals solidarity and may enable coordinated defense against India's overwhelming forces. Positive relationship maintained or slightly enhanced to ensure they view America as reliable ally during this crisis and don't seek separate peace that would leave America isolated facing India's military.
        - UpdatedTurn: 355
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Adjusting flavors for multi-front war against superior enemies. Nuke increased to 85 from 90 (we have 0 Uranium, still prioritized but realistic). UseNuke increased to 75 from 60 as nuclear weapons are now legitimate deterrents given India's aggression. Antiair maxed at 100 from 95 (air defense is critical given India's air power and our vulnerability). Air increased to 90 from 95 (sstill high but slightly lower than antiair priority). Offense reduced to 35 from 40 (defensive posture against all fronts). Defense/CityDefense/MilitaryTraining remain maxed - survival depends on strong defensive units and garrisons. Production reduced to 90 from 95 (happiness matters more in multiple wars). Science remains 100 (tech is survival). Gold increased to 80 from 85 (need for unit purchases/upgrades and uranium diplomacy). Diplomacy increased to 90 from 85 (critical for securing uranium and coordinating with allies Netherlands, Polynesia, Egypt). Happiness increased to 80 from 75 (combat penalty from unhappiness is devastating in multiple wars). Espionage increased to 90 from 85 (need intel on India's movements and nuclear capabilities).
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
        
        ### Flavors
        - Offense: 35
        - Defense: 100
        - Mobilization: 95
        - CityDefense: 100
        - MilitaryTraining: 100
        - Recon: 50

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 45
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 8 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Persia:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 331
          - Open Door: Wittenberg:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Panama City:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Sigtuna:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 291
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Ban Chiang:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 353
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 353
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - Proposer: India
          - Enact: Dutch Sphere of Influence: Jakarta:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.

              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.

        ### 190
        - Type: PlayerDoneTurn

        You, Washington (leader of America, Player 4), are making strategic decisions after turn 356.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Washington, leader of America (Opponent 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: America
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Minuteman, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Monitor, Replacing Ironclad (Requires technology: Industrialization)
        - Unique Building: Independence Hall, Replacing Royal Collection (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Building: Homestead, Replacing Stable (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Civilization Ability: Military Land Units start with the Sentry Promotion. Tile purchase cost is reduced by 25% and is reset upon founding a City. +20 Production when purchasing tiles, scaling with Era. Can purchase tiles owned by others.
      - Leader: Washington
      - OpponentID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the world (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      
      ## Policies
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Selecting New World Order (Level 1 Autocracy Tenet) as our next policy. Our civilization is currently Unhappy (39% happiness) which is causing a severe combat penalty across all fronts in our wars against Persia and Poland. New World Order provides -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all cities, and makes Constabularies/Police Stations construct 100% faster while granting them +3 Culture and +5 Production. This will immediately mitigate our happiness crisis, remove the devastating combat penalty, and significantly boost our production and culture across our 9 cities, stabilizing our empire for defense and counter-offensives.
        - Policy: New World Order
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Nuclear Fission
        - Rationale: Maintaining Nuclear Fission as our next technology. We have just completed the Manhattan Project in Boston (Turn 356) and successfully constructed a Quarry on our Uranium resource at X:15, Y:27. Researching Nuclear Fission is now our highest priority as it unlocks Atomic Bombs and Nuclear Plants. Having access to Atomic Bombs will give us a decisive strategic deterrent against India's massive conventional army (18,480 strength) and Poland's forces, ensuring our national survival and securing our borders.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - Flavors:
          - Air: 90
          - CityDefense: 100
          - MilitaryTraining: 100
          - UseNuke: 80
          - Diplomacy: 90
          - Science: 100
          - Defense: 95
          - Gold: 85
          - Happiness: 100
          - Antiair: 100
          - Production: 90
          - Nuke: 90
          - Naval: 85
          - Mobilization: 95
          - Offense: 45
          - Espionage: 90
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
      - rationale: Adjusting strategic flavors to focus on internal stability and defense. We increase Happiness to 100 (emergency priority) to solve our severe unhappiness crisis (39%) and remove the crippling combat penalty. We maintain Science at 100 and Nuke/UseNuke at 90/80 to capitalize on completing the Manhattan Project and researching Nuclear Fission. We set Offense to 45 (slightly up) to encourage finishing off Persia's last cities (Pasargadae at 2% HP, Persepolis at 56% HP) and reclaiming San Francisco with our dominant northern Navy (Naval boosted to 85). We keep Defense/CityDefense/Mobilization high to guard against Poland's land forces.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Assessing My Situation**
            
            My current score is 2033. I'm in the middle tier, behind Poland and India. I need to analyze the latest reports to identify strategic advantages and upcoming opportunities.
            
            **Analyzing Current Conflicts**
            
            I've been reviewing our active wars and realized a critical misunderstanding regarding India. Despite the defensive pact and their declaration of war against the Netherlands, we are not currently at war with India in the game engine, which is a significant relief given their military might. My immediate focus is on securing our existing conflicts and leveraging our recent Manhattan Project completion.
            
            **Securing Strategic Resources**
            
            I've confirmed our research path is optimal, aligning perfectly with our Manhattan Project completion. My immediate concern is acquiring Uranium; the worker's quarry on a uranium tile seems promising, though I'm verifying resource connection and availability for the next turn.
            
            **Addressing My Unhappiness**
            
            I've identified a critical happiness deficit hindering my military effectiveness. Fortunately, the "New World Order" tenet in Autocracy offers a powerful solution, directly reducing unhappiness and boosting city yields with existing buildings. My priority is to switch to this policy immediately.
            
            **Implementing Policy Solutions**
            
            I've confirmed that "New World Order" is the optimal policy choice to immediately address our critical unhappiness deficit, which is directly impacting combat effectiveness and city growth. I've decided against "Elite Forces" as it wouldn't negate the significant penalties from unhappiness. I'm also reassessing our relationship with Portugal and confirming our decisive victory against Persia.
            
            **Conquering Remaining Enemies**
            
            I've just realized we can capture Pasargadae and Persepolis from Persia, eliminating their threat entirely. I'm maintaining hostility to ensure the AI continues the offensive. Simultaneously, I'm assessing our ongoing conflict with Poland, noting their defenses and the loss of San Francisco.
            
            **Assessing Naval Superiority**
            
            My naval forces are overwhelmingly dominant in northern waters. I can leverage this power to bombard and capture Polish coastal cities, starting with reclaiming San Francisco. This will secure crucial resources and remove their northern threat.
            
            **Developing Atomic Deterrence**
            
            My immediate focus is on leveraging our Uranium acquisition to construct Atomic Bombs, ensuring a powerful deterrent against India and Poland. I've confirmed Nuclear Fission remains our next research target, and our current persona seems well-suited to our ongoing conflicts and potential future threats.
            
            **Reassessing Diplomatic Stances**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our relationships, especially concerning the Netherlands and Egypt. While Poland and Persia remain active threats, I'm prioritizing maintaining our alliance with the Netherlands as they face Indian aggression. I also noted Portugal has denounced us, prompting a cautious recalibration of our interactions.
            
            **Identifying Ideological Threats**
            
            I've noted that my opponent has adopted Order, a direct ideological clash with my Autocracy. This makes them a significant long-term threat, and I need to maintain vigilance. My immediate focus is on addressing our critical unhappiness, which is severely impacting combat effectiveness.
            
            **Prioritizing Atomic Arsenal**
            
            My focus is now squarely on developing atomic deterrence. I've secured the Uranium and have set Nuclear Fission as my next research target to expedite Atomic Bomb construction, aiming to neutralize immediate threats from India and Poland. I am also prioritizing diplomatic actions to solidify my alliance with the Netherlands.
            
            **Prioritizing Nuclear Research**
            
            My immediate focus is on researching Nuclear Fission to unlock Atomic Bombs, which will provide a critical deterrent against India and Poland. I've adjusted my flavors, prioritizing Happiness, Nuke, Defense, Science, and Production to manage our current crisis and secure a science victory while ensuring national survival.

          - toolName: set-research
          - input:
            - Technology: Nuclear Fission
            - Rationale: Maintaining Nuclear Fission as our next technology. We have just completed the Manhattan Project in Boston (Turn 356) and successfully constructed a Quarry on our Uranium resource at X:15, Y:27. Researching Nuclear Fission is now our highest priority as it unlocks Atomic Bombs and Nuclear Plants. Having access to Atomic Bombs will give us a decisive strategic deterrent against India's massive conventional army (18,480 strength) and Poland's forces, ensuring our national survival and securing our borders.

            - Rationale: Adjusting strategic flavors to focus on internal stability and defense. We increase Happiness to 100 (emergency priority) to solve our severe unhappiness crisis (39%) and remove the crippling combat penalty. We maintain Science at 100 and Nuke/UseNuke at 90/80 to capitalize on completing the Manhattan Project and researching Nuclear Fission. We set Offense to 45 (slightly up) to encourage finishing off Persia's last cities (Pasargadae at 2% HP, Persepolis at 56% HP) and reclaiming San Francisco with our dominant northern Navy (Naval boosted to 85). We keep Defense/CityDefense/Mobilization high to guard against Poland's land forces.
